\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\ell_{1}^{(j)}=\ell_{1}\\
\sum_{j=1}^{m}\ell_{2}^{(j)}=\ell_{2}\\
\sum_{j=1}^{m}\ell_{3}^{(j)}=\ell_{3}\end{subarray}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}A_{1}^{(j)},A_{2}^{(j)},A_{3}^{(j)}\subset[8]\ \textup{disjoint}\\
\absolutevalue{A_i^{(j)}}=2\ell_{i}^{(j)}\end{subarray}}\Tr(P_4\gamma_{A_1^{(j)}}\gamma_{A_2^{(j)}})\Tr(P_4\gamma_{A_2^{(j)}}\gamma_{A_3^{(j)}})\Tr(P_4\gamma_{A_3^{(j)}}\gamma_{A_1^{(j)}})\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\ell_{1}^{(j)}=\ell_{1}\\
\sum_{j=1}^{m}\ell_{2}^{(j)}=\ell_{2}\\
\sum_{j=1}^{m}\ell_{3}^{(j)}=\ell_{3}\end{subarray}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\mathcal{T}_{\ell_{1}^{(j)},\ell_{2}^{(j)},\ell_{3}^{(j)}}(P_{4}).

The generating function in the second line of Eq. (
506
) is

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell_{1},\ell_{2},\ell_{3}\geq 0\\
\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}+\ell_{3}\leq n\end{subarray}}\mathcal{T}_{\ell_{1},\ell_{2},\ell_{3}}(P_{4m})x^{\ell_{1}}y^{\ell_{2}}z^{\ell_{3}}

\displaystyle=\prod_{j=1}^{m}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell_{1}^{(j)},\ell_{2}^{(j)},\ell_{3}^{(j)}\geq 0\\
\ell_{1}^{(j)}+\ell_{2}^{(j)}+\ell_{3}^{(j)}\leq 4\end{subarray}}\mathcal{T}_{\ell_{1}^{(j)},\ell_{2}^{(j)},\ell_{3}^{(j)}}(P_{4})x^{\ell_{1}^{(j)}}y^{\ell_{2}^{(j)}}z^{\ell_{3}^{(j)}}\right)

(517)

\displaystyle=T[x,y,z]^{m}.

∎

For simplicity, we introduce a notation for the summation of symmetric monomials.

Definition 5

(Distinct symmetric monomial)
.

For nonnegative integers
a,b,c
, define

\operatorname{Sym}(x^{a}y^{b}z^{c})=\sum_{(p,q,s)\in\{\sigma(a,b,c)\}_{\sigma\in S_{3}}}x^{p}y^{q}z^{s},

(518)

where
(p,q,s)
goes over all possible permutations of
(a,b,c)
without
repetitions.

For example, we have
\operatorname{Sym}(x^{3})=x^{3}+y^{3}+z^{3}
and
\operatorname{Sym}(x^{2}yz)=x^{2}yz+xy^{2}z+xyz^{2}
. We now present the generating functions for state vectors
\ket{\eta_r}\bra{\eta_r}
.

Lemma 10

(Function for
\ket{\eta_r}\bra{\eta_r}
)
.

For
r\in\{0,1,2,3\}
,
n=4+r
, and the states
\ket{\eta_r}\bra{\eta_r}
defined in Eq. (
440
), the values of functions defined in Eq. (
462
) and Eq. (
463
) are given by

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=0}^{n}B_{\ell}(\ket{\eta_r}\bra{\eta_r})z^{\ell}

\displaystyle=P_{r}[z],

(519)

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell_{1},\ell_{2},\ell_{3}\geq 0\\
\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}+\ell_{3}\leq n\end{subarray}}\mathcal{T}_{\ell_{1},\ell_{2},\ell_{3}}(\ket{\eta_r}\bra{\eta_r})x^{\ell_{1}}y^{\ell_{2}}z^{\ell_{3}}

\displaystyle=T_{r}[x,y,z].

Explicitly,

\displaystyle P_{0}[z]=

\displaystyle P[z]=1+14z^{2}+z^{4},

(520)

\displaystyle P_{1}[z]=

\displaystyle(1+z)(1+14z^{2}+z^{4})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1+z+14z^{2}+14z^{3}+z^{4}+z^{5},

\displaystyle P_{2}[z]=

\displaystyle 1+15z^{2}+32z^{3}+15z^{4}+z^{6},

\displaystyle P_{3}[z]=

\displaystyle(1+z)(1+15z^{2}+32z^{3}+15z^{4}+z^{6})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1+z+15z^{2}+47z^{3}+47z^{4}+15z^{5}+z^{6}+z^{7}.

Moreover,

T_{0}[x,y,z]=T[x,y,z],\quad T_{1}[x,y,z]=(1-x-y-z)T[x,y,z],\quad T_{3}[x,y,z]=(1-x-y-z)T_{2}[x,y,z],

(521)

where

\displaystyle T_{2}[x,y,z]={}

\displaystyle 1+15\operatorname{Sym}(x^{2})-32\operatorname{Sym}(x^{3})+15\operatorname{Sym}(x^{4})+\operatorname{Sym}(x^{6})+90\operatorname{Sym}(x^{2}y^{2})

(522)

\displaystyle+15\operatorname{Sym}(x^{4}y^{2})+32\operatorname{Sym}(x^{3}y^{3})-120xyz+480\operatorname{Sym}(x^{2}yz)-120\operatorname{Sym}(x^{3}yz)

\displaystyle-480\operatorname{Sym}(x^{2}y^{2}z)+90x^{2}y^{2}z^{2},

P[z]
and
T[x,y,z]
are provided in Lemma
8
.

Proof of Lemma
10
.

For
r=1
,
\ket{\eta_1}=\ket{\mathrm{GHZ}_4}\otimes\ket{0}
. Using Eq. (
480
), the one-mode vacuum state vector
\ket{0}\bra{0}
contributes
(1+z)
and
(1-x-y-z)
to the generating function. Multiplying these by the
\mathrm{GHZ}_{4}
polynomials
P[z]
and
T[x,y,z]
proves the
r=1
identities. The same is for
r=3
, where the two factors are multiplied to
P_{2}[z]
and
T_{2}[x,y,z]
.

For
r=2
, a direct Majorana expansion of
\outerproduct{\eta_2}{\eta_2}
contains
64
nonzero monomials, all with coefficient magnitude
1/64
. Their degree distribution is

1\textup{ of degree }0,\quad 15\textup{ of degree }4,\quad 32\textup{ of degree }6,\quad 15\textup{ of degree }8,\quad 1\textup{ of degree }12.

(523)

This gives the formula for
P_{2}[z]
in Eq. (
520
). Substituting the same support set into the disjoint-set formula defining
\mathcal{T}
and grouping the terms by distinct exponent permutations gives Eq. (
522
), whose coefficients can be verified numerically.
∎

Lemma 11

(Function for
P_{n}
)
.

For the states
P_{n}
defined in Eq. (
441
), the values of functions defined in Eq. (
462
) and Eq. (
463
) are given by

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=0}^{n}B_{\ell}(P_{n})z^{\ell}

\displaystyle=P_{r}[z]P[z]^{m-1},

(524)

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell_{1},\ell_{2},\ell_{3}\geq 0\\
\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}+\ell_{3}\leq n\end{subarray}}\mathcal{T}_{\ell_{1},\ell_{2},\ell_{3}}(P_{n})x^{\ell_{1}}y^{\ell_{2}}z^{\ell_{3}}

\displaystyle=T_{r}[x,y,z]T[x,y,z]^{m-1},

where
P_{r}[z]
and
T_{r}[x,y,z]
are given in Lemma
10
,
P[z]
and
T[x,y,z]
are provided in Lemma
8
.

Proof of Lemma
11
.

The proof is the same blockwise convolution argument as in Lemma
9
. The only difference is that the first block is now the projector
\outerproduct{\eta_r}{\eta_r}
, whose generating function is provided in Lemma
10
, while the remaining
m-1
blocks are copies of
P_{4}
, provided in Lemma
8
.
∎

Noticeably, we show that the WE
2
-criterion in Theorem
7
detects all pure non-Gaussian states for
4\leq n\leq 7
qubits, by directly relating WE
2
-norm to the
fermionic antiflatness
(FAF) introduced in Ref.
99
. Let
\ket{\psi}
be a fixed-parity pure fermionic state on
n
modes, i.e.,
\psi=\outerproduct{\psi}{\psi}\in\mathcal{D}_{F}
, the elements of its correlation matrix
\Gamma
are defined by

\Gamma_{j,k}(\psi)=-\frac{i}{2}\bra{\psi}[\gamma_{j},\gamma_{k}]\ket{\psi},

(525)

and the first-order FAF is

\mathfrak{F}_{1}(\ket{\psi})=n-\frac{1}{2}\Tr\left(\Gamma(\psi)^{\mathrm{T}}\Gamma(\psi)\right).

(526)

For fixed-parity pure states, FAF is a faithful fermionic non-Gaussianity measure, as
\mathfrak{F}_{1}(\ket{\psi})\geq 0
, with equality holding if and only if
\ket{\psi}
is Gaussian
99
. Since the correlation matrix captures only the coefficients of Majorana products with degree 2, it fully determines WE
2
-norm for pure states with a small size, while WE
2
-norm still involves higher-degree Majorana coefficients to effectively detect non-Gaussianity of a general mixed state. We relate WE
2
-norm to FAF with a small system size for
4\leq n\leq 7
in Corollary
4
in the main text.

Proof of Corollary
4
.

We denote the parity operator as
Z^{\otimes n}=(-i)^{n}\gamma_{1}\gamma_{2}\cdots\gamma_{2n-1}\gamma_{2n}
, a valid fermionic state vector
\ket{\psi}
is an eigenstate of
Z^{\otimes n}
with a fixed parity
+1
or
-1
:

Z^{\otimes n}\ket{\psi}=\pm 1\ket{\psi}.

(527)

Recall the even Majorana sector purities for
\ell\in\{0,1,\cdots,n\}
of a pure state
\psi

(cf. Eq. (
463
)):

B_{\ell}(\psi)=\sum_{\absolutevalue{S}=2\ell}\absolutevalue{\bra{\psi}\widehat{\gamma}_S\ket{\psi}}^{2}.

(528)

Those Majorana sector purities obey some relations for a pure fermionic state
\psi
:

•

Boundary constraints:

B_{0}(\psi)=B_{n}(\psi)=1.

(529)

It can be seen from
\widehat{\gamma}_{\emptyset}=\mathbb{I}
and
\widehat{\gamma}_{[2n]}=(-i)^{n}Z^{\otimes n}
.

•

These coefficients are symmetric

B_{\ell}(\psi)=B_{n-\ell}(\psi)

(530)

towards the center.
Notice that
Z^{\otimes n}\widehat{\gamma}_{S}=\pm\widehat{\gamma}_{\bar{S}}
, where
\bar{S}=[2n]\setminus S
, we have

\absolutevalue{\bra{\psi}\widehat{\gamma}_S\ket{\psi}}^{2}=\absolutevalue{\bra{\psi}Z^{\otimes n}\widehat{\gamma}_S\ket{\psi}}^{2}=\absolutevalue{\bra{\psi}\widehat{\gamma}_{\bar{S}}\ket{\psi}}^{2},

(531)

which proves the above equation.

•

These coefficients sum to
2^{n}
:

\sum_{\ell=0}^{n}B_{\ell}(\psi)=2^{n}.

(532)

This can be seen from the expansion of the even state

\psi=2^{-n}\sum_{\ell=0}^{n}\sum_{\absolutevalue{S}=2\ell}\bra{\psi}\widehat{\gamma}_{S}\ket{\psi}\widehat{\gamma}_{S},

(533)

then use
\Tr(\psi^2)=1
and Eq. (
428
).

•

These coefficients are linear combinations of each other:

B_{\ell}(\psi)=2^{-n}\sum_{k=0}^{n}\sum_{j=0}^{2\ell}(-1)^{j}\binom{2k}{j}\binom{2n-2k}{2\ell-j}B_{k}(\psi).

(534)

Equivalently, recall that the binary Krawtchouk Polynomial is defined as
81

K_{r}(t;2n)=\sum_{j=0}^{r}(-1)^{j}\binom{t}{j}\binom{2n-t}{r-j},

(535)

the linear transformation of these coefficients is rewritten as

B_{\ell}(\psi)=2^{-n}\sum_{k=0}^{n}K_{2\ell}(2k;2n)B_{k}(\psi).

(536)

We use Eq. (
533
) and
\widehat{\gamma}_{S}\widehat{\gamma}_{T}\widehat{\gamma}_{S}=(-1)^{\absolutevalue{S\cap T}}\widehat{\gamma}_{T}
,

\displaystyle B_{\ell}(\psi)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\absolutevalue{S}=2\ell}\bra{\psi}\widehat{\gamma}_{S}\left(\ket{\psi}\bra{\psi}\right)\widehat{\gamma}_{S}\ket{\psi}

(537)

\displaystyle=2^{-n}\sum_{k=0}^{n}\sum_{\absolutevalue{T}=2k}\left(\sum_{\absolutevalue{S}=2\ell}(-1)^{\absolutevalue{S\cap T}}\right)\absolutevalue{\bra{\psi}\widehat{\gamma}_T\ket{\psi}}^{2}

\displaystyle=2^{-n}\sum_{k=0}^{n}\sum_{\absolutevalue{T}=2k}\sum_{j=0}^{2\ell}(-1)^{j}\binom{2k}{j}\binom{2n-2k}{2\ell-j}\absolutevalue{\bra{\psi}\widehat{\gamma}_T\ket{\psi}}^{2}

\displaystyle=2^{-n}\sum_{k=0}^{n}\sum_{j=0}^{2\ell}(-1)^{j}\binom{2k}{j}\binom{2n-2k}{2\ell-j}B_{k}(\psi).

Using Eq. (
529
), Eq. (
530
), Eq. (
532
), and Eq. (
534
), we can solve a set of linear equations for
\{B_{\ell}\}_{\ell=0}^{n}
. For
4\leq n\leq 7
, we can represent all other coefficients with

B_{1}(\psi)=\frac{1}{2}\Tr\left(\Gamma(\psi)^{\mathrm{T}}\Gamma(\psi)\right)=n-\mathfrak{F}_{1}(\ket{\psi}).

(538)

For
4\leq n\leq 7
, we explicitly calculate WE
2
-norm of
n
-qubit state
\psi
in Eq. (
465
)

\norm{\psi}_{P_{n},\mathrm{M}_{n},2}^{2}=\frac{1}{d^{2}}\sum_{\ell=0}^{n}\frac{B_{\ell}(P_{n})B_{\ell}(\psi)}{\binom{2n}{2\ell}},

(539)

with all the intermediate data provided in Table
3
. It is worth noting that
n\geq 8
will involve more variables than constraints, so that we cannot represent all
B_{\ell}
with
\mathfrak{F}_{1}
.

Proof of Theorem
8
and Corollary
5

Proof of Theorem
8
.

Let
d=2^{n}
, we now calculate the WE
2
-norm in Eq. (
95
).
Recall the seed operator defined in Eq. (
94
) for
1\leq\ell\leq n
,

\widehat{\gamma}_{T_{\ell}}=\prod_{j=1}^{\ell}Z_{j},\qquad T_{\ell}=[2\ell].

(541)

Using Eq. (
437
) and Eq. (
464
), we have

\displaystyle\norm{\rho}_{\widehat{\gamma}_{T_{\ell}},\mathrm{M}_{n},2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\underset{U\sim\mu_{\mathrm{M}_{n}}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\Tr(U^\dagger\widehat{\gamma}_{T_\ell} U\rho)^{2}\right]=\Tr\left[\Phi_{\mathrm{M}_{n}}^{(2)}\left(\widehat{\gamma}_{T_{\ell}}^{\otimes{2}}\right)\rho^{\otimes 2}\right]

(542)

\displaystyle=\binom{2n}{2\ell}^{-1}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\subseteq[2n]\\
\absolutevalue{S}=2\ell\end{subarray}}\Tr(\widehat{\gamma}_S\rho)^{2}

\displaystyle=\binom{2n}{2\ell}^{-1}B_{\ell}(\rho),

which proves Eq. (
95
). Notice that
\norm{\rho}_{\widehat{\gamma}_{T_{\ell}},\mathrm{M}_{n},2}
is the
L^{2}(\mu_{\mathrm{M}_{n}})
-norm of the function
f_{\rho}:\mathrm{M}_{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
,

f_{\rho}(U)=\Tr(U^\dagger\widehat{\gamma}_{T_\ell} U\rho),

(543)

since

\norm{f_\rho}_{L^{2}(\mu_{\mathrm{M}_{n}})}=\left(\int_{\mathrm{M}_{n}}\absolutevalue{\Tr(U^\dagger\widehat{\gamma}_{T_\ell} U\rho)}^{2}\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathrm{M}_{n}}\right)^{1/2}=\left(\underset{U\sim\mu_{\mathrm{M}_{n}}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\Tr(U^\dagger\widehat{\gamma}_{T_\ell} U\rho)^{2}\right]\right)^{1/2}.

(544)

It is convex by the Minkowski inequality, especially the convex Gaussian threshold

C_{\widehat{\gamma}_{T_{\ell}},\mathrm{M}_{n},2}=\sup_{\sigma\in\textup{Conv}(\mathcal{G}_{n})}\norm{\sigma}_{\widehat{\gamma}_{T_{\ell}},\mathrm{M}_{n},2}

(545)

is obtained at some pure Gaussian state
\phi\in\mathcal{G}_{n}
. Furthermore, all states in
\mathcal{G}_{n}
are in a single orbit under
\mathrm{M}_{n}
, we can evaluate the threshold value at
\outerproduct{0^n}{0^n}
. Inserting Eq. (
480
) to Eq. (
542
), we have

C_{\widehat{\gamma}_{T_{\ell}},\mathrm{M}_{n},2}^{2}=\norm{\ketbra{0^n}{0^n}}_{\widehat{\gamma}_{T_{\ell}},\mathrm{M}_{n},2}^{2}=\frac{\binom{n}{\ell}}{\binom{2n}{2\ell}},

(546)

which proves Eq. (
96
). In this way, the WE
2
-criterion with
\widehat{\gamma}_{T}
is

\norm{\rho}_{\widehat{\gamma}_{T_{\ell}},\mathrm{M}_{n},2}>C_{\widehat{\gamma}_{T_{\ell}},\mathrm{M}_{n},2}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad B_{\ell}(\rho)>\binom{n}{\ell}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\rho\notin\mathrm{Conv}(\mathcal{G}_{n}).

(547)

∎

We reformulate Corollary
5
as the following theorem and then prove it.

Theorem 14

(Central Majorana sector purity inequality)
.

For every fixed-parity pure state
\psi
with
n\geq 4
qubits,

B_{\left\lfloor n/2\right\rfloor}(\psi)-\binom{n}{\left\lfloor n/2\right\rfloor}\geq 0.

(548)

Moreover, equality holds if and only if
\psi
is a pure fermionic Gaussian state.

To prove Theorem
14
, we first introduce some notations and useful lemmas, with their proofs left to the end of this appendix.
For a series
A=(a_{0},a_{1},a_{2},\cdots)
, we define its generating function as the formal power series
F_{A}(z)
, where in general
z\in\mathbb{C}
,

F_{A}(z)=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}a_{j}z^{j}.

(549)

Equivalently, denote the coefficients
a_{j}
of the term
x^{j}
extracted from
F_{A}(z)
as

\left[z^{j}\right]F_{A}(z)=a_{j}.

(550)

For any fixed-parity pure state
\psi
, denote the generating function of its Majorana sector purities
B_{\ell}(\psi)
as

G_{\psi}(z)=\sum_{\ell=0}^{n}B_{\ell}(\psi)z^{\ell}.

(551)

We show
G_{\psi}(z)
can be rewritten in the following form.

Lemma 12

(Rewrite the generating function of Majorana sector purities)
.

For every fixed-parity pure state
\psi
, there exist real coefficients
c_{j}(\psi)
and
c_{0}(\psi)=1
, such that the generating function of its Majorana sector purities
B_{\ell}(\psi)
can be written as

G_{\psi}(z)=\sum_{\ell=0}^{n}B_{\ell}(\psi)z^{\ell}=\sum_{j=0}^{\left\lfloor n/4\right\rfloor}c_{j}(\psi)(1+z)^{n-4j}\left(z(1-z)^{2}\right)^{j}.

(552)

Recall that by Eq. (
480
),

B_{\ell}(\outerproduct{0^n}{0^n})=\binom{n}{\ell},

(553)

its generating function is

G_{\outerproduct{0^n}{0^n}}(z)=(1+z)^{n}.

(554)

We calculate the difference in Eq. (
548
), for any fixed-parity pure state
\psi
,

\displaystyle B_{\left\lfloor n/2\right\rfloor}(\psi)-\binom{n}{\left\lfloor n/2\right\rfloor}

\displaystyle=B_{\left\lfloor n/2\right\rfloor}(\psi)-B_{\left\lfloor n/2\right\rfloor}(\outerproduct{0^n}{0^n})

(555)

\displaystyle=\left[z^{\left\lfloor n/2\right\rfloor}\right]\left(G_{\psi}(z)-G_{\outerproduct{0^n}{0^n}}(z)\right)

\displaystyle=\left[z^{\left\lfloor n/2\right\rfloor}\right]\sum_{j=1}^{\left\lfloor n/4\right\rfloor}c_{j}(\psi)(1+z)^{n-4j}\left[z(1-z)^{2}\right]^{j}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{\left\lfloor n/4\right\rfloor}\left[z^{\left\lfloor n/2\right\rfloor-j}\right]c_{j}(\psi)(1+z)^{n-4j}(1-z)^{2j}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{\left\lfloor n/4\right\rfloor}c_{j}(\psi)e_{n,j},

where in the third line we
have used
c_{0}(\psi)=1
and the
j=0
term in Eq. (
552
) collides with
G_{\outerproduct{0^n}{0^n}}(z)
. In the last line, we define

e_{n,j}=\left[z^{\left\lfloor n/2\right\rfloor-j}\right](1+z)^{n-4j}(1-z)^{2j}.

(556)

We introduce another set of coefficients through a linear transformation of
e_{n,j}
, and show that each of them is strictly positive.

Lemma 13

(
\beta_{n,p}
is strictly positive)
.

For
1\leq p\leq\left\lfloor n/4\right\rfloor
, define
\beta_{n,p}
from a linear transformation of
e_{n,j}
as

\beta_{n,p}=\sum_{j=1}^{p}(-1)^{j}\frac{2p}{p+j}\binom{p+j}{p-j}\frac{e_{n,j}}{4^{n-3j}},

(557)

then
\beta_{n,p}>0
.

Recall the binary Krawtchouk polynomial defined in Eq. (
535
),

K_{r}(t;2n)=\sum_{j=0}^{r}(-1)^{j}\binom{t}{j}\binom{2n-t}{r-j},

(558)

we further define the function for any fixed-parity pure state
\psi
,

S_{r}(\psi)=\sum_{\ell=0}^{n}(-1)^{\ell}K_{r}(2\ell;2n)B_{\ell}(\psi).

(559)

We can show that this function is non-negative for any
0\leq r\leq 2n
.

Lemma 14

(
S_{r}(\psi)
is non-negative)
.

For every fixed-parity pure state
\psi
and every
0\leq r\leq 2n
,

S_{r}(\psi)\geq 0.

(560)

Furthermore,
S_{r}(\psi)
is related to the coefficients
c_{j}(\psi)
in Eq. (
552
) through the following lemma.

Lemma 15

(Relation between
S_{r}(\psi)
and
c_{j}(\psi)
)
.

Let
1\leq p\leq\left\lfloor n/4\right\rfloor
, we have

S_{n-4p}(\psi)=(-1)^{p}\sum_{j=p}^{\left\lfloor n/4\right\rfloor}4^{n-3j}\binom{2j}{j-p}c_{j}(\psi).

(561)

At last, we rewrite the difference in Eq. (
548
) using
\beta_{n,p}
and
S_{r}(\psi)
.

Lemma 16

(Rewrite the central Majorana sector purity difference)
.

For every fixed-parity pure state
\psi
,

B_{\left\lfloor n/2\right\rfloor}(\psi)-\binom{n}{\left\lfloor n/2\right\rfloor}=\sum_{p=1}^{\left\lfloor n/4\right\rfloor}\beta_{n,p}S_{n-4p}(\psi),

(562)

where
\beta_{n,p}
is given in Eq. (
557
) and
S_{r}(\psi)
is defined in Eq. (
559
).

We can now prove Theorem
14
and finally finish the proof of Theorem
8
in the main text.

Proof of Theorem
14
.

Combining Lemma
16
with Lemma
13
and Lemma
14
, we have

B_{\left\lfloor n/2\right\rfloor}(\psi)-\binom{n}{\left\lfloor n/2\right\rfloor}\geq 0.

(563)

The equality holds if and only if
S_{n-4p}(\psi)=0
for all
1\leq p\leq\left\lfloor n/4\right\rfloor
. In this case, from Lemma
15
,
p=\left\lfloor n/4\right\rfloor
implies
c_{\left\lfloor n/4\right\rfloor}(\psi)=0
. Inserting it to
p=\left\lfloor n/4\right\rfloor-1
gives
c_{\left\lfloor n/4\right\rfloor-1}(\psi)=0
. Using this iteratively until
p=1
, we have

c_{1}(\psi)=c_{2}(\psi)=\cdots=c_{\left\lfloor n/4\right\rfloor}(\psi)=0.

(564)

According to Eq. (
552
) in Lemma
12
, it is equivalent to

G_{\psi}(z)=(1+z)^{n},\qquad B_{1}(\psi)=n.

(565)

Especially, the FAF

\mathfrak{F}_{1}(\ket{\psi})=n-B_{1}(\psi)=0,

(566)

which indicates
\psi
is a pure fermionic Gaussian state.
∎

We provide the proofs for each of the above lemmas.

Proof of Lemma
12
.

For the generating function of the Majorana sector purities

G_{\psi}(z)=\sum_{\ell=0}^{n}B_{\ell}(\psi)z^{\ell},

(567)

we consider a generalized homogeneous function, defined by

F_{\psi}(x,y)=\sum_{\ell=0}^{n}B_{\ell}(\psi)x^{2n-2\ell}y^{2\ell}.

(568)

In this way,
G_{\psi}(z)=F_{\psi}(x,y)
by setting
x^{2}=1
and
y^{2}=z
. We show
F_{\psi}(x,y)
has four symmetries

\displaystyle\text{① }F_{\psi}(x,y)

\displaystyle=F_{\psi}(-x,y),

\displaystyle\qquad\text{② }F_{\psi}(x,y)

\displaystyle=F_{\psi}(x,-y),

(569)

\displaystyle\text{③ }F_{\psi}(x,y)

\displaystyle=F_{\psi}(y,x),

\displaystyle\qquad\text{④ }F_{\psi}(x,y)

\displaystyle=F_{\psi}\left(\frac{x+y}{\sqrt{2}},\frac{x-y}{\sqrt{2}}\right).

Symmetries ① and ② hold since both
x
and
y
have only even power in
F_{\psi}(x,y)
. Symmetry ③ is from
B_{\ell}(\psi)=B_{n-\ell}(\psi)
by Eq. (
530
). To show symmetry ④,

\displaystyle F_{\psi}\left(\frac{x+y}{\sqrt{2}},\frac{x-y}{\sqrt{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=2^{-n}F_{\psi}(x+y,x-y)

(570)

\displaystyle=2^{-n}\sum_{k=0}^{n}B_{k}(\psi)(x+y)^{2n-2k}(x-y)^{2k}

\displaystyle=2^{-n}\sum_{k=0}^{n}B_{k}(\psi)\sum_{a=0}^{2n-2k}\binom{2n-2k}{a}x^{2n-2k-a}y^{a}\sum_{b=0}^{2k}\binom{2k}{b}(-1)^{b}x^{2k-b}y^{b}

\displaystyle=2^{-n}\sum_{k=0}^{n}B_{k}(\psi)\sum_{\ell=0}^{n}\sum_{j=0}^{2\ell}\binom{2k}{j}\binom{2n-2k}{2\ell-j}(-1)^{j}x^{2n-2\ell}y^{2\ell}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=0}^{n}B_{\ell}(\psi)x^{2n-2\ell}y^{2\ell}=F_{\psi}(x,y),

In the third line, we have used the binomial expansion. In the fourth line we relabel

a=2\ell-j
and
b=j
, so the summation becomes

\sum_{a=0}^{2n-2k}\sum_{b=0}^{2k}\rightarrow\sum_{\ell=0}^{n}\sum_{j=0}^{\min\{2\ell,2k\}}.

(571)

We can safely set the upper limit of
j
to be
2\ell
since
\binom{2k}{j}
appears in the term. In the last line,
we have used the linear transformation between Majorana sector purities by the binary Krawtchouk polynomial in Eq. (
534
):

B_{\ell}(\psi)=2^{-n}\sum_{k=0}^{n}\sum_{j=0}^{2\ell}(-1)^{j}\binom{2k}{j}\binom{2n-2k}{2\ell-j}B_{k}(\psi).

(572)

We then prove any
2n
-degree homogeneous polynomial
F(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}[x,y]
satisfying the above four symmetries can be represented as

F(x,y)=\sum_{j=0}^{\left\lfloor n/4\right\rfloor}c_{j}\xi^{n-4j}\eta^{j},\qquad\text{with }\xi=x^{2}+y^{2},\eta=x^{2}y^{2}(x^{2}-y^{2})^{2}.

(573)

First, from symmetries ① and ②,
F
contains only even powers of both variables
x
and
y
, there exists a polynomial
F_{1}
, such that

F(x,y)=F_{1}(x^{2},y^{2}).

(574)

By symmetry ③,
F_{1}
is invariant under exchange of
x
and
y
, there exists a polynomial
F_{2}
, such that

F(x,y)=F_{1}(x^{2},y^{2})=F_{2}(x^{2}+y^{2},x^{2}y^{2})=F_{2}(\xi,x^{2}y^{2}).

(575)

We then use symmetry ④, under transformation
(x,y)\rightarrow((x+y)/\sqrt{2},(x-y)/\sqrt{2})
, we have

F(x,y)=F_{2}(\xi,x^{2}y^{2})=F_{2}\left(\xi,\frac{\xi^{2}}{4}-x^{2}y^{2}\right).

(576)

So
F_{2}
is invariant under reflection of the second argument about
\xi^{2}/8
, there exists a polynomial
F_{3}
, such that

F(x,y)=F_{3}\left(\xi,\left(\frac{\xi^{2}}{8}-x^{2}y^{2}\right)^{2}\right)=F_{3}\left(\xi,\frac{\xi^{4}}{64}-\frac{\eta}{4}\right).

(577)

Thus, there exists coefficients
b_{k}
and
c_{j}
, such that

\displaystyle F(x,y)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{\left\lfloor n/4\right\rfloor}b_{k}\xi^{n-4k}\left(\frac{\xi^{4}}{64}-\frac{\eta}{4}\right)^{k}

(578)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{\left\lfloor n/4\right\rfloor}b_{k}\xi^{n-4k}\sum_{j=0}^{k}\binom{k}{j}\left(\frac{\xi^{4}}{64}\right)^{k-j}\left(-\frac{\eta}{4}\right)^{j}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{\left\lfloor n/4\right\rfloor}\sum_{k=j}^{\left\lfloor n/4\right\rfloor}b_{k}\binom{k}{j}64^{j-k}(-4)^{-j}\xi^{n-4j}\eta^{j}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{\left\lfloor n/4\right\rfloor}c_{j}\xi^{n-4j}\eta^{j}.

Especially, there exist coefficients
c_{j}(\psi)\in\mathbb{R}
, such that

F_{\psi}(x,y)=\sum_{j=0}^{\left\lfloor n/4\right\rfloor}c_{j}(\psi)\xi^{n-4j}\eta^{j}=\sum_{j=0}^{\left\lfloor n/4\right\rfloor}c_{j}(\psi)\left(x^{2}+y^{2}\right)^{n-4j}\left(x^{2}y^{2}(x^{2}-y^{2})^{2}\right)^{j},

(579)

Setting
x^{2}=1
and
y^{2}=z
, we have

G_{\psi}(z)=\sum_{j=0}^{\left\lfloor n/4\right\rfloor}c_{j}(\psi)\left(1+z\right)^{n-4j}\left(z(1-z)^{2}\right)^{j},

(580)

which is Eq. (
552
). Furthermore, we have

c_{0}(\psi)=\left[z^{0}\right]G_{\psi}(z)=B_{0}(\psi)=1

(581)

from Eq. (
529
). We finish the proof of Lemma
12
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
13
.

To prove (cf. Eq. (
557
))

\beta_{n,p}=\sum_{j=1}^{p}(-1)^{j}\frac{2p}{p+j}\binom{p+j}{p-j}\frac{e_{n,j}}{4^{n-3j}}>0,

(582)

it is sufficient to prove for
1\leq j\leq\left\lfloor n/4\right\rfloor
,
(-1)^{j}e_{n,j}>0
, with (cf. Eq. (
556
))

e_{n,j}=\left[z^{\left\lfloor n/2\right\rfloor-j}\right](1+z)^{n-4j}(1-z)^{2j}.

(583)

Relabel
K=n-2j
and
k=\left\lfloor n/2\right\rfloor-j
, then

e_{n,j}=\left[z^{k}\right](1+z)^{K-2j}(1-z)^{2j}.

(584)

Kotice that for a polynomial
f(z)\in\mathbb{C}[z]
, the coefficient of
z^{k}
can be calculated using Cauchy’s coefficient formula

\left[z^{k}\right]f(z)=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\oint_{\absolutevalue{z}=1}\frac{f(z)}{z^{k+1}}\mathrm{d}z=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}f(e^{i\theta})e^{-ik\theta}\mathrm{d}\theta.

(585)

Inserting
f(z)=(1+z)^{K-2j}(1-z)^{2j}
, we discuss the following two cases and show both lead to
(-1)^{j}e_{n,j}>0
.

•

If
K=2k
is even,

\displaystyle(-1)^{j}e_{n,j}

\displaystyle=\frac{(-1)^{j}}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}(1+e^{i\theta})^{K-2j}(1-e^{i\theta})^{2j}e^{-ik\theta}\mathrm{d}\theta

(586)

\displaystyle=\frac{(-1)^{j}}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\left(2e^{i\theta/2}\cos\left(\frac{\theta}{2}\right)\right)^{2k-2j}\left(-2ie^{i\theta/2}\sin\left(\frac{\theta}{2}\right)\right)^{2j}e^{-ik\theta}\mathrm{d}\theta

\displaystyle=\frac{2^{K}}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\cos^{2k-2j}\left(\frac{\theta}{2}\right)\sin^{2j}\left(\frac{\theta}{2}\right)\mathrm{d}\theta

\displaystyle>0,

where in the last line, we have used all terms in the integrand are even powers, and are not always zero for
\theta\in[0,2\pi)
.

•

If
K=2k+1
is odd,

\displaystyle(-1)^{j}e_{n,j}

\displaystyle=\frac{(-1)^{j}}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}(1+e^{i\theta})^{K-2j}(1-e^{i\theta})^{2j}e^{-ik\theta}\mathrm{d}\theta

(587)

\displaystyle=\frac{(-1)^{j}}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\left(2e^{i\theta/2}\cos\left(\frac{\theta}{2}\right)\right)^{2k+1-2j}\left(-2ie^{i\theta/2}\sin\left(\frac{\theta}{2}\right)\right)^{2j}e^{-ik\theta}\mathrm{d}\theta

\displaystyle=\frac{2^{K}}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}e^{i\theta/2}\cos^{2k+1-2j}\left(\frac{\theta}{2}\right)\sin^{2j}\left(\frac{\theta}{2}\right)\mathrm{d}\theta

\displaystyle=\frac{2^{K}}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\cos^{2k+2-2j}\left(\frac{\theta}{2}\right)\sin^{2j}\left(\frac{\theta}{2}\right)\mathrm{d}\theta

\displaystyle>0.

where in the fourth line, we have used
e_{n,j}\in\mathbb{R}
, so
e_{n,j}=\mathrm{Re}[e_{n,j}]
and
\mathrm{Re}[e^{i\theta/2}]=\cos\left(\theta/2\right)
. In the last line, we have used all terms in the integrand are even powers, and are not always zero for
\theta\in[0,2\pi)
.

∎

Proof of Lemma
14
.

Recall

S_{r}(\psi)=\sum_{\ell=0}^{n}(-1)^{\ell}K_{r}(2\ell;2n)B_{\ell}(\psi),

(588)

where the binary Krawtchouk polynomial is defined as

K_{r}(t;2n)=\sum_{j=0}^{r}(-1)^{j}\binom{t}{j}\binom{2n-t}{r-j}.

(589)

For a subset
S\subseteq[2n]
, define
\varepsilon_{j}(S)\in\{\pm 1\}
for
j\in[2n]
and the Hermitian observable
\Omega_{S}
as

\varepsilon_{j}(S)=\begin{cases}-1,\quad&j\in S,\\
1,&j\notin S,\end{cases}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\Omega_{S}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}T\subseteq[2n]\\
\absolutevalue{T}\text{ is even}\end{subarray}}\prod_{j\in T}\varepsilon_{j}(S)\gamma_{j}\otimes\gamma_{j}.

(590)

Notice that
[\gamma_{j}\otimes\gamma_{j},\gamma_{k}\otimes\gamma_{k}]=0
for
j,k\in[2n]
, so the product
\prod_{j\in T}
is unambiguous. Specifically, when
T=\emptyset
, we set

\prod_{j\in\emptyset}\varepsilon_{j}(S)\gamma_{j}\otimes\gamma_{j}=\mathbb{I}.

(591)

We show that first
\Omega_{S}\geq 0
and then

S_{r}(\psi)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\subseteq[2n]\\
\absolutevalue{S}=r\end{subarray}}\bra{\psi}^{\otimes 2}\Omega_{S}\ket{\psi}^{\otimes 2}\geq 0.

(592)

We use the identities

\displaystyle\prod_{j=1}^{2n}\left(\mathbb{I}+\varepsilon_{j}(S)\gamma_{j}\otimes\gamma_{j}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{T\subseteq[2n]}\prod_{j\in T}\varepsilon_{j}(S)\gamma_{j}\otimes\gamma_{j},

(593)

\displaystyle\prod_{j=1}^{2n}\left(\mathbb{I}-\varepsilon_{j}(S)\gamma_{j}\otimes\gamma_{j}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{T\subseteq[2n]}(-1)^{\absolutevalue{T}}\prod_{j\in T}\varepsilon_{j}(S)\gamma_{j}\otimes\gamma_{j},

which can be seen from the following. Expand the ordered product by distributivity.
For each factor one chooses either the identity term or the term

\varepsilon_{j}(S)\gamma_{j}\otimes\gamma_{j}
. Such choices are in one-to-one
correspondence with subsets
T\subseteq[2n]
, where
T
records the
indices for which the nontrivial term is chosen.
We have

\displaystyle\Omega_{S}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{T\subseteq[2n]}\left(1+(-1)^{\absolutevalue{T}}\right)\prod_{j\in T}\varepsilon_{j}(S)\gamma_{j}\otimes\gamma_{j}

(594)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(\sum_{T\subseteq[2n]}\prod_{j\in T}\varepsilon_{j}(S)\gamma_{j}\otimes\gamma_{j}+\sum_{T\subseteq[2n]}(-1)^{\absolutevalue{T}}\prod_{j\in T}\varepsilon_{j}(S)\gamma_{j}\otimes\gamma_{j}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{2n}\left(\mathbb{I}+\varepsilon_{j}(S)\gamma_{j}\otimes\gamma_{j}\right)+\prod_{j=1}^{2n}\left(\mathbb{I}-\varepsilon_{j}(S)\gamma_{j}\otimes\gamma_{j}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\geq 0.

In the last line, we have used the fact that the eigenvalues of
\varepsilon_{j}(S)\gamma_{j}\otimes\gamma_{j}
are
\pm 1
, so the terms
\left(\mathbb{I}+\varepsilon_{j}(S)\gamma_{j}\otimes\gamma_{j}\right)
and
\left(\mathbb{I}-\varepsilon_{j}(S)\gamma_{j}\otimes\gamma_{j}\right)
are positive semi-definite. They commute with each other, so their product is also positive semi-definite.
Fix any
T\subseteq[2n]
with
\absolutevalue{T}=2\ell
, we have

\displaystyle\widehat{\gamma}_{T}\otimes\widehat{\gamma}_{T}

\displaystyle=(-i)^{\absolutevalue{T}(\absolutevalue{T}-1)}\gamma_{T}\otimes\gamma_{T}

(595)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{\ell}\prod_{j\in T}\gamma_{j}\otimes\gamma_{j},

where we have used
\widehat{\gamma}_{T}=(-i)^{\absolutevalue{T}(\absolutevalue{T}-1)/2}\gamma_{T}
in Eq. (
430
).
And

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\subseteq[2n]\\
\absolutevalue{S}=r\end{subarray}}\prod_{j\in T}\varepsilon_{j}(S)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\subseteq[2n]\\
\absolutevalue{S}=r\end{subarray}}(-1)^{\absolutevalue{S\cap T}}=\sum_{j=0}^{r}(-1)^{j}\binom{2\ell}{j}\binom{2n-2\ell}{r-j}=K_{r}(2\ell;2n),

(596)

where in the middle equality we count
j=\absolutevalue{S\cap T}
and use
\absolutevalue{T}=2\ell
. Finally, inserting the definition of
B_{\ell}(\psi)
in Eq. (
528
), we obtain

\displaystyle S_{r}(\psi)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=0}^{n}(-1)^{\ell}K_{r}(2\ell;2n)B_{\ell}(\psi)

(597)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=0}^{n}(-1)^{\ell}\sum_{\absolutevalue{T}=2\ell}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\subseteq[2n]\\
\absolutevalue{S}=r\end{subarray}}\prod_{j\in T}\varepsilon_{j}(S)\absolutevalue{\bra{\psi}\widehat{\gamma}_T\ket{\psi}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\subseteq[2n]\\
\absolutevalue{S}=r\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}T\subseteq[2n]\\
\absolutevalue{T}\text{ is even}\end{subarray}}\prod_{j\in T}\varepsilon_{j}(S)\bra{\psi}^{\otimes 2}\left(\gamma_{j}\otimes\gamma_{j}\right)\ket{\psi}^{\otimes 2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\subseteq[2n]\\
\absolutevalue{S}=r\end{subarray}}\bra{\psi}^{\otimes 2}\Omega_{S}\ket{\psi}^{\otimes 2}\geq 0.

∎

Proof of Lemma
15
.

Let
1\leq p\leq\left\lfloor n/4\right\rfloor
, we want to show

S_{n-4p}(\psi)=(-1)^{p}\sum_{j=p}^{\left\lfloor n/4\right\rfloor}4^{n-3j}\binom{2j}{j-p}c_{j}(\psi),

(598)

where
c_{j}(\psi)
is determined in Eq. (
552
) in Lemma
12
. We calculate the generating function of
K_{r}(2\ell;2n)
:

\displaystyle\sum_{r=0}^{2n}K_{r}(2\ell;2n)t^{r}

\displaystyle=\sum_{r=0}^{2n}\sum_{j=0}^{r}(-1)^{j}\binom{2\ell}{j}\binom{2n-2\ell}{r-j}t^{r}

(599)

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{j=0}^{2\ell}\binom{2\ell}{j}(-t)^{j}\right)\left(\sum_{k=0}^{2n-2\ell}\binom{2n-2\ell}{k}t^{k}\right)

\displaystyle=(1-t)^{2\ell}(1+t)^{2n-2\ell},

where in the second line, we have used
k=r-j
.
Define and then calculate the generating function of
S_{r}(\psi)
:

\displaystyle S_{\psi}(t)

\displaystyle=\sum_{r=0}^{2n}S_{r}(\psi)t^{r}

(600)

\displaystyle=\sum_{r=0}^{2n}\sum_{\ell=0}^{n}(-1)^{\ell}K_{r}(2\ell;2n)B_{\ell}(\psi)t^{r}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=0}^{n}(-1)^{\ell}B_{\ell}(\psi)\sum_{r=0}^{2n}K_{r}(2\ell;2n)t^{r}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=0}^{n}(-1)^{\ell}B_{\ell}(\psi)(1-t)^{2\ell}(1+t)^{2n-2\ell}

\displaystyle=(1+t)^{2n}\sum_{\ell=0}^{n}B_{\ell}(\psi)\left(-\left(\frac{1-t}{1+t}\right)^{2}\right)^{\ell}

\displaystyle=(1+t)^{2n}G_{\psi}\left(-\left(\frac{1-t}{1+t}\right)^{2}\right),

where in the last step, we
have used
G_{\psi}
is the generating function of
B_{\ell}(\psi)
. By Eq. (
552
) in Lemma
12
, there exist real coefficients
\left\{c_{j}(\psi)\right\}
with
c_{0}(\psi)=1
, such that

G_{\psi}(z)=\sum_{j=0}^{\left\lfloor n/4\right\rfloor}c_{j}(\psi)(1+z)^{n-4j}\left(z(1-z)^{2}\right)^{j}.

(601)

Inserting into Eq. (
600
), we have

\displaystyle S_{\psi}(t)

\displaystyle=(1+t)^{2n}G_{\psi}\left(-\left(\frac{1-t}{1+t}\right)^{2}\right)

(602)

\displaystyle=(1+t)^{2n}\sum_{j=0}^{\left\lfloor n/4\right\rfloor}c_{j}(\psi)\left(\frac{4t}{(1+t)^{2}}\right)^{n-4j}(-1)^{j}\left(\frac{1-t}{1+t}\right)^{2j}\left(\frac{2(1+t^{2})}{(1+t)^{2}}\right)^{2j}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{\left\lfloor n/4\right\rfloor}(-1)^{j}4^{n-3j}c_{j}(\psi)t^{n-4j}(1-t^{4})^{2j}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{\left\lfloor n/4\right\rfloor}\sum_{k=0}^{2j}(-1)^{j+k}4^{n-3j}c_{j}(\psi)\binom{2j}{k}t^{n-4j+4k}.

Finally, for
1\leq p\leq\left\lfloor n/4\right\rfloor
, we obtain

\displaystyle S_{n-4p}(\psi)

\displaystyle=\left[t^{n-4p}\right]S_{\psi}(t)

(603)

\displaystyle=\left[t^{n-4p}\right]\sum_{j=0}^{\left\lfloor n/4\right\rfloor}\sum_{k=0}^{2j}(-1)^{j+k}4^{n-3j}c_{j}(\psi)\binom{2j}{k}t^{n-4j+4k}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{\left\lfloor n/4\right\rfloor}(-1)^{p}4^{n-3j}c_{j}(\psi)\binom{2j}{j-p},

which is Eq. (
598
) after noticing that the terms for
j<p
in the summation vanish.
∎

Proof of Lemma
16
.

It is equivalent to prove

\sum_{j=1}^{\left\lfloor n/4\right\rfloor}c_{j}(\psi)e_{n,j}=\sum_{p=1}^{\left\lfloor n/4\right\rfloor}\beta_{n,p}S_{n-4p}(\psi).

(604)

Inserting Eq. (
561
) in Lemma
15
into the right-hand side, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{p=1}^{\left\lfloor n/4\right\rfloor}\beta_{n,p}S_{n-4p}(\psi)

\displaystyle=\sum_{p=1}^{\left\lfloor n/4\right\rfloor}\beta_{n,p}(-1)^{p}\sum_{j=p}^{\left\lfloor n/4\right\rfloor}4^{n-3j}\binom{2j}{j-p}c_{j}(\psi)

(605)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{\left\lfloor n/4\right\rfloor}c_{j}(\psi)4^{n-3j}\sum_{p=1}^{j}\binom{2j}{j-p}(-1)^{p}\beta_{n,p}.

Notice that
\beta_{n,p}
in Eq. (
557
) can be regarded as a linear transformation from
e_{n,j}
as

\beta_{n,p}=\sum_{j=1}^{p}(-1)^{j}\frac{2p}{p+j}\binom{p+j}{p-j}\frac{e_{n,j}}{4^{n-3j}},

(606)

it is sufficient to prove that the inverse transformation is given by

e_{n,j}=4^{n-3j}\sum_{p=1}^{j}\binom{2j}{j-p}(-1)^{p}\beta_{n,p},

(607)

then plug it into Eq. (
605
) finishes the proof of Lemma
16
.
Define the vectors
\vec{u}:=(u_{j})_{j=1}^{\left\lfloor n/4\right\rfloor}
and
\vec{v}:=(v_{p})_{p=1}^{\left\lfloor n/4\right\rfloor}
by

u_{j}=\frac{e_{n,j}}{4^{n-3j}},\qquad v_{p}=(-1)^{p}\beta_{n,p}.

(608)

The transformation from
e_{n,j}
to
\beta_{n,p}
in Eq. (
606
) can be represented as
\vec{v}=N\vec{u}
by matrix
N\in\mathcal{M}_{\left\lfloor n/4\right\rfloor}(\mathbb{R})
with elements

N_{p,j}=\begin{cases}(-1)^{p-j}\frac{2p}{p+j}\binom{p+j}{p-j},\qquad&p\geq j,\\
0,&p<j.\end{cases}

(609)

Define the matrix
M\in\mathcal{M}_{\left\lfloor n/4\right\rfloor}(\mathbb{R})
with elements

M_{j,p}=\begin{cases}\binom{2j}{j-p},\qquad&j\geq p,\\
0,&j<p,\end{cases}

(610)

then Eq. (
607
) reduces to
\vec{u}=M\vec{v}
, which is equivalent to
MN=\mathbb{I}
.
We notice that both
N
and
M
are lower-triangle matrices with diagonal elements all equal to
1
. It is sufficient to show
NM=\mathbb{I}
, so that
N
and
M
are matrix inverses to each other. We calculate the matrix element

\displaystyle\left(NM\right)_{p,r}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{\left\lfloor n/4\right\rfloor}N_{p,j}M_{j,r}

(611)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=r}^{p}(-1)^{p-j}\frac{2p}{p+j}\binom{p+j}{p-j}\binom{2j}{j-r}

\displaystyle=2p\sum_{j=r}^{p}(-1)^{p-j}\frac{(p+j-1)!}{(p-j)!(j-r)!(j+r)!}.

•

If
p<r
, then the index set in the above summation is empty, so
\left(NM\right)_{p,r}=0
.

•

If
p=r
, then

\left(NM\right)_{p,r}=2r\frac{(2r-1)!}{(2r)!}=1.

(612)

•

If
p>r
, relabel
k=j-r
and
K=p-r\geq 1
. We have

\displaystyle\left(NM\right)_{p,r}

\displaystyle=2p\sum_{j=r}^{p}(-1)^{p-j}\frac{(p+j-1)!}{(p-j)!(j-r)!(j+r)!}

(613)

\displaystyle=2p\sum_{k=0}^{K}(-1)^{K-k}\frac{(p+r+k-1)!}{(K-k)!k!(2r+k)!}

\displaystyle=\frac{2p}{K}\sum_{k=0}^{K}(-1)^{K-k}\binom{K}{k}\binom{p+r+k-1}{K-1}.

Consider taking the finite difference of a polynomial
P(x)\in\mathbb{R}[x]
, defined as

\Delta P(x)=P(x+1)-P(x).

(614)

We show that for
L\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, the
L
-fold action results in

\Delta^{L}P(x)=\sum_{l=0}^{L}(-1)^{L-l}\binom{L}{l}P(x+l).

(615)

For
L=1
and
2
, it is easily verified, as by the linearity of
\Delta
,

\Delta^{2}P(x)=\Delta P(x+1)-\Delta P(x)=P(x+2)-2P(x+1)+P(x).

(616)

Assume Eq. (
615
) holds for
L
, then

\displaystyle\Delta^{L+1}P(x)

\displaystyle=\Delta^{L}P(x+1)-\Delta^{L}P(x)

(617)

\displaystyle=\sum_{l^{\prime}=0}^{L}(-1)^{L-l^{\prime}}\binom{L}{l^{\prime}}P(x+1+l^{\prime})-\sum_{l=0}^{L}(-1)^{L-l}\binom{L}{l}P(x+l)

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=0}^{L+1}(-1)^{L+1-l}\left(\binom{L}{l-1}+\binom{L}{l}\right)P(x+l)

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=0}^{L+1}(-1)^{L+1-l}\binom{L+1}{l}P(x+l),

where in the third line we relabel
l^{\prime}=l-1
in the first summation in the second line and use
\dbinom{L}{-1}=\dbinom{L}{L+1}=0
. By induction, Eq. (
615
) holds for any
L\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
. Let
x=0
, we have

\Delta^{L}P(0)=\sum_{l=0}^{L}(-1)^{L-l}\binom{L}{l}P(l).

(618)

Notice that
\deg(\Delta(x^{a}))=a-1
for
a\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, so in general, if
\deg(P(x))\geq 1
, the degree decreases at least by one after finite difference,

\deg(\Delta P(x))\leq\deg(P(x))-1.

(619)

Especially, if
\deg(P(x))<L
, then
\Delta^{L}P(x)=0
. Combined with Eq. (
618
), we obtain the identity for any polynomial
P(x)
with
\deg(P)<L
,

\sum_{l=0}^{L}(-1)^{L-l}\binom{L}{l}P(l)=0.

(620)

Back to the calculation of Eq. (
613
), we define the polynomial of
x
with degree
K-1
as

Q(x)=\binom{x+p+r-1}{K-1}=\frac{1}{(K-1)!}\prod_{l=1}^{K-1}(x+p+r-l),

(621)

and recognize
Q(k)=\dbinom{p+r+k-1}{K-1}
. Thus by Eq. (
620
),

\displaystyle\left(NM\right)_{p,r}

\displaystyle=\frac{2p}{K}\sum_{k=0}^{K}(-1)^{K-k}\binom{K}{k}\binom{p+r+k-1}{K-1}

(622)

\displaystyle=\frac{2p}{K}\sum_{k=0}^{K}(-1)^{K-k}\binom{K}{k}Q(k)

\displaystyle=0.

So we prove Eq. (
607
) and complete the proof of Lemma
16
.
∎
